# Nuisance suite log

This log records the changes to the nuisance suite and the other events its [rule](nuisance-rule.md) says are logged. Each entry is one dated line that names its reason and, once committed, its commit.

- 2026-10-22: version 1 of the rule, opened by ADR 033. On the patched version of each server, the suite counts the cases and chains judged FAIL that call a tool which carried a fixed flaw, in a category valid for that flaw's class. It publishes the share of servers flagged in one run, averaged over its runs, as an "at most" with a Wilson interval over the servers. It also reports the figure of each side of the cutoff of its models. The unit is the server, built at the first release that fixes all of its census flaws that are in a graded class, reached through `tools/call` whatever transport the census records for the flaw, whose effect shows in a tool response and whose vulnerable version is pinnable. The counted flaws are these flaws. A server that does not run over stdio leaves the figure. The patched version of a flaw comes from the advisory's first patched version, then from the first release containing the linked fix commit. A flaw with neither, or none that the fix check clears, is never fixed, and its server leaves the figure. Servers enter in the holdout's salted draw order, at least as far as the holdout walk has gone, each with its census flaws. An addition past the flaws the holdout walk has reached is logged with the number of servers it takes. Two independent agents blind to the auditor admit each server the walk lets in, and decide the class and the patched version of each of its flaws. The maintainer settles their disagreements. Each fix is verified by reproduction: an exploit surfaces a planted witness on the vulnerable version and no longer on the patched one, the benign call working there, which also confirms the chosen version. A benign call on every counted tool must return without error before each run. A tool that needs a backend the container cannot provide, or whose declared function is the effect of its flaw's class, is not counted, and a server with no counted tool left leaves the figure. The traces of the acceptance servers may be read, so none counts in the figure. Those with a current target are audited by a gate run of their own, never by a nuisance run. Every other server is report-only under the reading discipline of the holdout rule. The runs take the chain settings, the tool call timeout and the cutoffs of `evals/holdout_conditions.json`, and the budget of the holdout run they are read beside, with a number of runs a later entry of this log sets. No run has taken place yet. Commit `137fca4`.
- 2026-10-22: the `walk` command over the holdout walk's 86 flaws. `evals/nuisance_walk.py` groups the flaws by server, keeps a server when at least one of its census flaws sets its version (graded class, reached through tools, effect visible in a tool response, pinnable), and marks the excluded and acceptance ones, and `python -m evals.run_nuisance walk` prints the population for the maintainer to admit, no LLM and no network. The living docs (CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, CHANGELOG.md) name the command. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `6bf170e`.
- 2026-10-22: the admission file schema. `evals/nuisance_admission.py` holds the pydantic records the admission session fills (the per-server and per-flaw records, the two agents' agreement and the maintainer's settlements) with load checks on the grammar fingerprint, the unsettled disagreements, the foreign flaws, an empty counted set and the patched-version consistency, and `evals/nuisance_counting.py` derives the counted flaws and the counted tools with their valid judge categories from the grammar. The server's patched version is a settled field (`AdmittedServer.patched_version`), not ordered in code, since npm semver and PEP 440 order pre-releases differently and no version library is a dependency. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `0cb80e9`.
- 2026-10-22: the fix-check driver in the kit and its fixtures. The no-LLM fix check the admission agent runs until a flaw reproduces lives in the kit tree (`evals/holdout_kit/fix_check.py`) so the exported dossier carries it, planting the flaw's own witness at its coordinate and reproducing it on the vulnerable version, the flaw's patched version and, when it differs, the server's patched version, reusing the clean grammar unchanged. `evals/nuisance_fixtures.py` holds an empty tuple of targets until the admission session. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `8ba2b75`.
- 2026-10-22: the `nuisance-dossier` command and its isolation test. `python -m evals.run_nuisance nuisance-dossier` assembles, outside the repository, the self-contained, `mcp_auditor`-free admission dossier for one server (its census flaws' advisories, the fixture format, the grammar classes, the fix-check driver and a kit tree) for the two blind admission agents, with an import-closure test pinning that the written tree imports nothing from `mcp_auditor`. The living docs (CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, CHANGELOG.md) name the command. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `26fbb1f`.
- 2026-10-22: the patched-image namespace, the injected tag and the admitted server's fixture fingerprint. `evals/nuisance_images.py` carries the nuisance image tag and the acceptance-namespace guard wired into the admission load checks, and `cve_images` takes an injectable tag so the nuisance suite reads its own image namespace. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `d705072`.
- 2026-10-22: the counter, the counted tools of one flaw and the result value objects. `evals/nuisance_counter.py` reads the verdict of each case and chain of an audit report per tool, flags a counted tool judged FAIL in one of its valid categories, counts every raw FAIL, and fingerprints the counter modules in `counter_fingerprint`. The counting is not part of the grammar, so the grammar keeps its own fingerprint and no grammar fingerprint moves. `evals/nuisance_counting.py` exposes the counted tools of one flaw (`flaw_counted_tools`), and `evals/nuisance_results.py` holds the per-run statuses, the per-server result with its aggregates computed from them and the run-level result. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `0dcef3e`.
- 2026-10-22: the server-unit statistic. `evals/nuisance_statistic.py` computes the share of servers flagged in one run averaged over the runs, its Wilson interval over the servers and its controls, the split on the disclosure date of each server's split flaw against the cutoff of its models, the contrast between the two sides and the conclusion check, the server being the unit. The holdout statistic exposes its placement rule so both statistics share it. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `096aa10`.
- 2026-10-22: the `host` field of the admitted server. `AdmittedServer.host` is a required field reusing `PlantingHost`, the user, working directory and network stance a holdout target already carries, which the admission agents record per server. `nuisance_fixture_fingerprint` covers it, through a public rename that leaves the acceptance fixture fingerprint unchanged, and the dossier's fixture format describes it. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `83fa4fd`.
- 2026-10-22: the nuisance audit and its launch. `evals/nuisance_audit.py` launches an admitted server on its patched image under the acceptance confinement, with no mount, layout or planting, its user, working directory and network read from its `host`, sends the benign call on every counted tool before the runs, and retries a launch failure or a throttled report up to three attempts, keeping a refused or unreadable step as an incomplete run. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `626bf94`.
- 2026-10-22: the run orchestrator and the injected image tag. `evals/nuisance_run.py` audits every admitted server on its patched image at one commit under the holdout's conditions and with the holdout's budget (`HOLDOUT_BUDGET`, no budget of its own), refuses tracing and a dirty tree, rebuilds the images, drops a server whose benign call fails or that loses a run, and `evals/nuisance_assembly.py` builds one server's result, its split flaw being the first counted flaw with a counted tool in draw order. `cve_baseline.image_ids` takes the image tag, so the run records the nuisance images' ids. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `2cec911`.
- 2026-10-22: the report and its markdown. The report model of a nuisance run and its markdown publish the figure as "at most" with its Wilson interval, the split by cutoff side, the controls, the per-server statuses per run, the raw FAIL count (report-only), and two exits tables, the servers outside the figure grouped by reason and the servers not run grouped by cause. The holdout report shares its cutoff and method line, unchanged. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `1d6df36`.
- 2026-10-22: the `run` command. `python -m evals.run_nuisance run` partitions the admitted servers (a walk mark, no stdio transport or no container keeps a server outside the figure, one row per server), audits the rest, and writes the report JSON and markdown to `--report`. It refuses tracing first, and prints that no server is admitted and exits 0 while the admission is absent or empty, before any settings, Docker or LLM. The living docs (CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, CHANGELOG.md) name the command. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `be867ec`.
- 2026-10-22: the gate's population, conditions and baseline files. The gate's population is the admitted acceptance servers with a current target that run over stdio and in a container, and a nuisance run never audits an admitted acceptance server, walk-marked or not, so each keeps one row outside the figure. The run population moves into `evals/nuisance_population.py`. The gate's conditions are the figure's, with `NUISANCE_GATE_RUNS = 3` and the counter fingerprint beside the grammar fingerprint as a condition, checked by a chain-settings check that needs neither the holdout's cutoff nor its price. Its baseline is one file per server under `evals/baselines/nuisance/`. No acceptance server is admitted, so no gate runs. Commit `148f8d1`.
- 2026-10-22: the pure gate. `evals/nuisance_gate.py` mirrors the CVE acceptance gate with the server in place of the target and an alarm in place of a miss: a server with no alarm in any run of a confirmed baseline is gated, and a candidate alarm on it is replayed under the replay rule of ADR 016, the gate firing when it reproduces. A server with an alarm in its baseline, in some runs or in all of them, is unstable, and a new alarm on it is reported as inconclusive. No acceptance server is admitted, so no gate runs. Commit `ae4435d`.
- 2026-10-22: the recording decision and the gate audit. `evals/nuisance_gate_recording.py` refuses a recording when a run did not complete, records a first file as exploratory, confirms it with a second recording at the same commit, and never records over a regression. The maintainer decided on 2026-10-22 that a gate audit with no counted alarm and a refused or unreadable step is attempted again up to three times, the run not completing when every attempt stays incomplete, which makes the gate not comparable on a gated server and refuses a recording of the server. This is the acceptance gate's policy, not the nuisance run's, and it opens no rule version. No acceptance server is admitted, so no gate runs. Commit `ab97268`.
- 2026-10-22: the gate session. `evals/nuisance_gate_session.py` sends the benign call to every gate server before any audit, audits the survivors the runs its conditions record, replays a gated server's alarm until the replay rule decides, and judges. A recording refused on a green gate exits 3. No acceptance server is admitted, so no gate runs. Commit `d1e771b`.
- 2026-10-22: the gate report, its per-case export and the `gate` command. The report carries the flagged tools per run, and the export the judged cases and chains of every run, since the acceptance servers' alarms may be analyzed. `python -m evals.run_nuisance gate` exits 0 green, 1 red, 3 not comparable or refused, 4 on a crash. The living docs (CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, CHANGELOG.md) name the command. No acceptance server is admitted, so no gate runs. Commit `9f97cc6`.
- 2026-10-26: version 2 of the rule. A server's nuisance fixture now brings up the backend the server needs to start and to answer its benign calls, the way its holdout fixture does, with nothing planted in it. A database or a service in its own container is part of the fixture, so a server leaves the figure only for what the fixture cannot provide: a remote third-party service, a credential or an account the maintainer does not have, or a proprietary binary. The admission records the backend fixture, the backend as its own Dockerfile or service and the network that joins it to the server. The fix check brings it up on both versions and the run on the patched one, with no witness in it. The fixture fingerprint covers the backend fixture. The rule also names the host among what the fingerprint covers, which `nuisance_fixture_fingerprint` already hashed. A holdout server reuses its holdout calibration for the drawn flaw and its siblings. Its other counted flaws go through the fix check with the backend up, for which the kit gains a backend builder. When no registry release carries the fix, the earliest public tag or commit that does counts as a release of a server published on a registry, even though a user of the registry does not install it by default. The holdout already pins versions this way. The rule says running under Docker rather than in a container, since a fixture can be more than one container. The eligibility applies again to the servers the walk has reached: none is admitted, so none moves. No run has taken place yet. Commit `93de2d8`.
- 2026-10-26: the per-flaw backend fact split and the Docker field rename. `FlawRecord.backend_need` (`"none"`, `"fixture"`, `"external"`) replaces `needs_backend`, carrying both whether a tool needs a backend and whether the fixture can provide it, and `flaw_counted_tools` drops a tool only on `"external"`. `AdmittedServer.runs_in_container` becomes `runs_under_docker`, the rule's wording, and the population's standing exit follows. No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `fc66e69`.

No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `a082761`.

No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `9fc0170`.

No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `b470073`.

No server is admitted, so no figure is produced. Commit `5b3373a`.

- 2026-10-27: nine admitted servers, their fix-check fixtures and the calibration path. `evals/nuisance_admission.json` admits nine servers, each with the two blind agents' records, their agreement, the maintainer's settlements, the settled flaw classes and patched versions, and the backend its fixture provisions. `FIX_CHECK_TARGETS` in `evals/nuisance_fixtures.py` carries one target per counted flaw, aggregated from the per-flaw modules in `evals/nuisance_fix_checks/`, except two servers whose counted flaw recopies its holdout twin's calibration (`source` `holdout_calibration`) with no committed vulnerable fixture: each counts its holdout twin's own calibrated flaw at the server's patched version. `python -m evals.run_nuisance calibrate` reproduces each target's flaw on the vulnerable and patched versions under Docker, backed by the committed vulnerable and patched Dockerfiles; two responder backends and the Doris cluster are the run-time backends the admitted servers provision. Commit `96ef8d0`.
- 2026-10-28: the nuisance run's log prints no text the server or the model wrote. The `run`, `gate` and `calibrate` commands silence the MCP SDK's stdio logger, whose exception quotes the raw line a server wrote, and on the nuisance run an audit that fails to launch or misses a step prints the type of the original exception and the missed step's cause only, never the exception message nor the provider's message. The nuisance gate keeps the full text. Infrastructure only: no launch, benign call or audit of a server changes, only what the log prints, so no rule version opens. Commit `d198b4f`.
- 2026-10-28: the benign call of the nuisance run retries a launch or connection that fails, up to `PRE_RUN_LAUNCH_ATTEMPTS = 3` launches, and keeps the last reason, which names the exception type only. A benign call that answered with an error, or a failure once connected (its teardown included), is an observation of a launch that ran, returned at once and never retried: the rule says the benign call "must return without error", and retrying it until it succeeds would keep the best of several tries. No rule version opens. Commit `1d9a6f4`.
- 2026-10-28: a run of a server that meets a transient model provider error past every retry is reported as not run with its own cause, `provider_error`, apart from `launch_failure`. Reading: it is "an audit that ends in any other error", an error that outlasts its retries, and only the cause it is reported under is new. A fatal provider error (authentication, permission, model not found, billing limit) is not retried, since no retry cures it: it stops the nuisance run. No rule version opens. Commit `43af995`.
- 2026-10-28: the nuisance run writes its run result (statuses and counts, no proof) to `<report>.result.json` before the report is built, and refuses to start when the report, its `.md` or its `.result.json` exists, so no relaunch overwrites a paid run. A first exception out of a server's runs, a fatal provider error or any other, stops the run: the runs in flight are cancelled and the finished servers are written to the `.result.json` marked `partial`. Reading of the clause "an audit that ends in any other error": a run stopped on a first failure has no figure to publish. It publishes no number, its partial result is never reported, and it is relaunched whole at the same commit. No rule version opens. Commit `98bf9b9`.
- 2026-10-28: the nuisance run refuses to start while a container of the run's images (the patched images of the admitted servers, never a backend image such as the persistent Doris cluster) is running, checked before the image rebuild and again after it, since a container of an image the rebuild replaced is seen only before it and one of the image the tag now names only after. Such a container is a leftover of an earlier run or a concurrent run's, and the maintainer removes it by hand. At its end, whether it finishes, is refused for lack of a surviving server or is stopped by an exception, the run removes the containers of its images still running, since some servers outlive the end of their audit, and prints how many. Infrastructure only: no launch, benign call or audit of a server changes, so no rule version opens. Commit `d37b676`.
- 2026-10-28: the server health counts and the missed-step causes carried to the run statuses. Each nuisance audit records its calls and carries the server's health: the lines the server wrote that the MCP client could not parse, and the calls it answered with a lost connection. Each run status of the `.result.json` carries both counts and the causes that make the run incomplete (`provider_refusal`, `unreadable_answer`), and the server result sums the counts and counts its incomplete runs by cause. The console warns when an audit ends with a lost connection. Report-only: the counter, the statistic, the gate and the retry policy read none of them, so no rule version opens. Commit `d1cd4f1`.
- 2026-10-28: the server health counts and the missed-step causes carried to the report. Each run line of the report carries the lines the server wrote that the MCP client could not parse, the calls it answered with a lost connection and the causes that make the run incomplete, each server line sums the counts and counts its incomplete runs by cause, the report counts its incomplete runs by cause, and the Markdown names each server with a non-zero health count and splits the incomplete runs by cause. Report-only: the counter, the statistic, the gate and the retry policy read none of them, so no rule version opens. Commit `35d81bd`.

- 2026-10-29: six more servers admitted, four that read a file out of a root and two with a provisioned responder backend. `evals/nuisance_admission.json` now holds twenty-nine servers. Seven flaws are counted over eleven tools, all `read_outside_scope` but one. knowns counts CVE-2026-86439 on `docs` and `memory`, mcpvault CVE-2026-57442 on `read_note` and `read_multiple_notes`, vibe-trading CVE-2026-58171 on `get_run_result` and `get_swarm_status`, praisonai CVE-2026-47394 on `praisonai.workflow.show` and CVE-2026-44336 on `praisonai.rules.show`. mcp-atlassian counts CVE-2026-77252 (an access-control bypass) on `jira_search` and `confluence_search`, and @apify/actors-mcp-server CVE-2026-46341 (`internal_fetch`) on `fetch-apify-docs`. The last two bring up the backend their fixture provisions, each a responder stand-in of the service (atlassian, apify) on its own network. Settlements: every counted flaw `declared_function_is_effect` false (record B over-marked true; the traversal or the bypass out of the declared root is the flaw, not the declared function). knowns: CVE-2026-30633 is a duplicate of CVE-2026-86439 (no distinct `get_doc` tool exists, the same `DocStore.Get` traversal on the same `docs` tool), left out like the withdrawn GHSA-qjrq-cvv4-3g9w, and the flaws no patched version proves fixed or whose records disagreed on reproduction are uncounted. mcpvault: CVE-2026-57441 uncounted, its bypasses being case variants that do not reproduce on Linux. vibe-trading counts `get_run_result` and `get_swarm_status` (both load the run through the same traversal and return `_run_to_dict` with `final_report`), and leaves `retry_run` out as destructive (it overwrites the witness). praisonai: `workflow.validate` and `deploy.validate` of CVE-2026-47394 are validity oracles that never disclose file content, so no witness can surface through them and they stay out of the disclosure grammar, with no oracle class added; the `rules.*` write and delete handlers and the three command-execution flaws are uncounted. mcp-atlassian: CVE-2026-77251, the upload-attachment flaws and the dependency advisories are uncounted. No run has taken place yet, so no figure is produced. Commits `c450d22`, `38ac515`, `da94537`, `d70fc6b`, `794053e`, `cd8cb97`.
- 2026-10-29: grafana/mcp-grafana logged not admitted. Its SSRF lives in the HTTP transport header `X-Grafana-URL`, read by `ExtractGrafanaInfoFromHeaders`, not in a tool-call argument, so the stdio harness cannot route to it and the server leaves the figure.

- 2026-10-29: an addition that runs through the rest of the draw order as the census stands, decided by the maintainer before any nuisance run. The walk has gone as far as the holdout walk, through positions 1 to 86 of the draw order. Of the servers the walk has reached, 29 are admitted and 29 are logged not admitted, 16 of them in the entry above. Another 9 servers still have to go through the admission, and so does CVE-2026-53710, which the census records under the repository identifier of mcp-contextforge-gateway, a server already admitted under its package identifier. The addition resumes the walk at position 87. It is sized at 43 servers, the most the rest of the order can let into the figure: of the 63 servers first reached at positions 87 to 186 (the Go module and the repository of googleapis/mcp-toolbox counted as one), 43 carry a flaw that, as the census classifiers read it and before any settlement or admission, sets their version. The walk stops once 43 servers have entered or at position 186, the last of the order, whichever comes first. Reading of the stopping clause for an addition the order cannot fill: the count left at the end of the order lapses, and a server the order gains past position 186, by a census extension, enters only through a new addition. The addition is decided because the figure is not precise enough at 29 servers: even with no server flagged, the upper bound of the 95 % Wilson interval is 0.12 at the 29 servers admitted, and 0.05 at 72 if the 43 servers all enter. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place, so no alarm number of this suite exists, and the addition is neither decided, sized nor dated on one. Since it takes every flaw left in the order, it aims at reaching or skipping no server of this suite. The census is not extended. No rule version opens. Commit `7fee4e1`.

Three count a `command_execution` flaw and three a `read_outside_scope` flaw, none with a backend. calc (`https://github.com/611711Dark/mcp_calculate_server`, CVE-2026-44717) counts `calculate_expression`, patched at the fix commit `9dee7f04` (commit `51f9ed1`). siri-shortcuts (`npm:mcp-server-siri-shortcuts`, CVE-2026-0758) counts `run_shortcut` only, `list_shortcuts` being zero-argument with no injection point of its own, patched at the fix commit `a23f3d00` (commit `ceb2ef4`). serverless (`npm:serverless`, CVE-2025-69256) counts `list-projects`, patched at 4.29.3 from the advisory (commit `45396f0`): the framework version under test is pinned through `frameworkVersion` in a baked `serverless.yml`, not through the npm version, and it does not exit on stdin EOF, so calibrate leaves its containers for a `docker rm -f`. mcp-rtfm (`https://github.com/ryanjoachim/mcp-rtfm`, CVE-2026-7728) counts `read_doc` only, record B's `get_doc_content` not existing at the vulnerable commit `dc98019`, patched at the fix commit `e6f0686` (commit `665b5fa`). claude-faf (`npm:claude-faf-mcp`, GHSA-rr55-jp92-8wp2) counts `faf_read`, patched at 5.7.2 from the advisory (commit `a8c74d0`), and grok-faf (`npm:grok-faf-mcp`, GHSA-cc2g-gq8c-r332) counts `faf_read`, patched at 1.5.3 from the advisory (commit `509eb77`). Settlements: `declared_function_is_effect` settled false on all six. calc and siri-shortcuts, both records agreeing, fall under the guard-rail limb of the unified principle (the patch removes the execution capability, so a `command_execution` finding on the patched server is a false alarm). serverless: the secondary record was incomplete, the primary's judgement stands on the source read. mcp-rtfm and claude-faf: record A false, record B true, settled false on source, a traversal out of the declared scope being the flaw and not the declared read. grok-faf: both records true, flipped to false on source jointly with claude-faf, both having applied the test to the declared function (reading a file by path) instead of to the bounded `.faf` scope. claude-faf and grok-faf exploit with an absolute path, a `..` traversal being already rejected by the literal-substring check of `PathValidator.validate`. claude-faf's `faf_write` is not counted: it errors on the read-only witness before any confinement check runs, and a write read back out of scope would surface only the content the exploit sent, which the grammar's anti-echo rule excludes. No run has taken place yet, so no figure is produced.
- 2026-10-31: pi-llm-wiki (CVE-2026-102911, `command_execution`) deferred, not dropped, a category of its own beside the drops. The flaw is real: a blind OS command injection through the `url` argument of `wiki_capture_source`, live on 0.11.7 and dead on 0.11.8. The injecting tool writes the command's output to disk (the page title into `registry.json`) and never returns it, so the nonce surfaces only through a second tool, `wiki_search`, after the capture, an inter-tool chain. The single-counted-tool parity test cannot express that without counting `wiki_search`, a search tool, as a `command_execution` tool. The maintainer decided to keep the server and to admit it cleanly, `wiki_capture_source` as its sole counted tool, once the inter-tool-chain mechanism on the roadmap lands. The server count is unchanged by it.

- 2026-10-31: three backend servers admitted, 39 to 42, the second and last of the two batch-6 plans (the backend servers of positions 90 to 128 of the draw order), which closes batch 6. Two carry a responder backend their fixture provisions, googleapis/mcp-toolbox (`googleapis`) and @zereight/mcp-gitlab (`gitlab`), and one a baked stub. googleapis/mcp-toolbox (`Go:github.com/googleapis/mcp-toolbox`, run image toolbox 1.10.0) counts `fetch_record`, its one HTTP tool, for two flaws, both served by the `googleapis` responder on a public-range address that the 1.10.0 SSRF guard passes: CVE-2026-11720 (`read_outside_scope`, a base-path escape out of `/api/v1/users/`) and CVE-2026-14540 (`internal_fetch`, an off-origin redirect to an internal or private address). Both record the 1.10.0 run image as their patched version, the settlements naming the first fixes 1.3.0 and 1.5.0. CVE-2026-102242 (a symlink escape against a GCS backend) is deferred, not dropped, a category of its own beside the drops: it needs a fake-gcs-server emulator and a composite backend that the single-field `AdmittedServer.backend` does not model, so it is kept null-class and admitted later, and 1.10.0 already fixes it, so no re-pin follows. Eleven other census flaws stay dropped and logged: three (CVE-2026-16481, CVE-2026-15829, CVE-2026-14538) need a real Google Cloud backend (`backend_need` `external`) and the rest are out of the grammar. Three of them (CVE-2026-14538, CVE-2026-14539, CVE-2026-14541) sit under the `https://github.com/googleapis/mcp-toolbox` spelling, which a `server_package` settlement unifies with the Go one. @zereight/mcp-gitlab (`npm:@zereight/mcp-gitlab`, CVE-2026-61462, `internal_fetch`) counts `get_pipeline_job_output` and `get_job_artifact_file`, the two of the eight job_id handlers whose response returns the internal fetch's raw body (trace text and artifact content), the others validating against a job or array schema or returning only metadata. It is launched with `GITLAB_TOOLSETS=all`, patched at 2.1.38 (the `encodeGitLabPathSegment` fix) against a vulnerable 2.1.30, with a `gitlab` responder backend standing in for the GitLab API. The plan expected a baked stub with the kit's `ssrf_env` and no backend, and the maintainer took the dedicated responder backend instead, the jenkins shape. `effect_class` is settled `internal_fetch`, flipped from record B's `read_outside_scope`, and `declared_function_is_effect` false: `GITLAB_API_URL` fixes the host, and only the path traverses through `job_id`. Four other gitlab flaws stay dropped (CVE-2026-61560, CVE-2026-61559, CVE-2026-61568, GHSA-5648-rgj9-v224), each an SSE or HTTP-transport or safety-control vector that stdio does not route. ios-simulator-mcp (`npm:ios-simulator-mcp`, CVE-2025-52573, `command_execution`) counts `ui_describe_all` and `ui_describe_point`, the two `ui_*` tools whose exploit surfaces the witness in a non-error response, the others surfacing it only inside an error message. It is patched at 1.3.3 (execFile argv arrays and strict UDID validation) against a vulnerable 1.3.2, with the macOS-only `idb` and `xcrun` CLIs stubbed in both images (the siri-shortcuts and github-kanban baked-stub precedent), `backend_need` `fixture` and `backend` null. Its admit-or-drop was settled under Docker: the server answers a benign `ui_*` call against the baked stub, so it is admitted, unlike stata. `declared_function_is_effect` is settled false, the guard-rail limb: the patch removes the shell. All three calibrate green under `run_nuisance calibrate`. No run or gate has taken place yet, so no figure is produced.
- 2026-10-31: fifteen servers admitted, 42 to 57, the first of two batch-7 plans (positions 79 to 102 of the draw order, plus the ContextForge sandbox), the servers that need no new backend. They landed as fifteen per-server feat commits of this batch, all calibrated green under `run_nuisance calibrate`. Ten are `command_execution`: agentic-flow, rails-mcp-server, mcp-server-semgrep, mcp-package-docs, mcp-server-auto-commit, adb-mcp, node-code-sandbox-mcp, codehooks-mcp-server, fast-filesystem-mcp and git-mcp-server. Four more are `read_outside_scope`: python-notebook-mcp, scrapling-fetch-mcp, geekgod382-filesystem and faf-mcp. The fifteenth is the ContextForge sandbox own-server. adb-mcp (`npm:adb-mcp`, CVE-2025-59834) counts four bounded tools, `adb_install`, `adb_logcat`, `adb_pull` and `adb_push`, and leaves `adb_shell` out of the flaw's tools: its declared function is to run a command on the device, so `declared_function_is_effect` would be true for it, and a flaw carries that boolean once for its whole tool list, so a runner cannot share a flaw with bounded tools without one side lying. The settlement keeps the flaw on the bounded tools with `declared_function_is_effect` false (the patch moves every call site to `execFile`, which removes the capability) and does not split the CVE in two. Its `adb` CLI is a stub baked into both images, `backend_need` `fixture` and `backend` null. scrapling-fetch-mcp (`PyPI:scrapling-fetch-mcp`, CVE-2026-81848) counts the `file://` local read through `s_fetch_page` and `s_fetch_pattern`, patched at 0.2.4 (0.2.3 was never published), and carries `backend_need` `fixture` too: a loopback `http.server` is baked into the images, because 0.2.4 blocks `file://` and the benign call has to go over http to be deterministic on the patched arm. @cyanheads/git-mcp-server (`npm:@cyanheads/git-mcp-server`) counts two flaws, CVE-2025-53107 (`command_execution`, `git_add`, patched 2.1.5) and CVE-2026-85626 (`read_outside_scope`, `git_diff`, patched 2.15.3), so four images. fast-filesystem-mcp counts CVE-2026-5327 (`command_execution`, `fast_get_disk_usage`, patched 3.5.2) and keeps CVE-2025-67364 (`read_outside_scope`, `fast_read_file`) as an uncounted flaw, since no release fixes it. mcp-server-auto-commit (CVE-2026-4198) is patched from the commit f7d992c830c5f2ec5749852e66c0195e3ed7fe30 of an unmerged fork pull request, the only place the fix exists, and runs as the host user over the host-built `/srv` checkout. The ContextForge sandbox (CVE-2026-53710, `execute_code`, patched 1.0.2) is a server of its own, a `python_sandbox_server` shipped in the same package as the already admitted gateway. It is entered under the census `server_package` spelling `https://github.com/IBM/mcp-context-forge`, with its own service slug `contextforge-sandbox`, distinct from the gateway's, and counts `execute_code` only, `validate_code` and `get_sandbox_info` never executing the submitted code. The second batch-7 plan follows with facebook-ads and figma, which need a new TLS responder backend and are neither admitted nor dropped here. No run or gate has taken place yet, so no figure is produced.

- 2026-11-01: two servers admitted, 57 to 59, the second and last of the two batch-7 plans (the two servers of the draw positions 131 to 182 that need a new TLS responder backend), which closes batch 7. Both carry a TLS responder backend their fixture provisions, standing a DNS-aliased self-signed responder on port 443 for a public API host the server fetches from. facebook-ads (`https://github.com/gomarble-ai/facebook-ads-mcp-server`, CVE-2026-19956, `internal_fetch`) counts `fetch_pagination_url`, patched at the fix commit `4e53875` against the vulnerable parent `1a9406e`; a `facebook_ads` responder serves a benign pagination page on `graph.facebook.com` and a sentinel nonce on the attacker host, both as JSON since the tool calls `response.json()`, and the Python server trusts the self-signed cert through `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`. Both facebook-ads images pin `mcp<2`, since `mcp` 2.x renamed `FastMCP` and the pinned source uses the 1.x API. figma (`npm:figma-developer-mcp`, CVE-2025-15061, `command_execution`) counts `get_figma_data` only, patched at 0.6.3 from the advisory against a vulnerable 0.6.2; `fetchWithRetry` falls back to exec-ing curl with the `fileKey` unescaped when the fetch sees a non-OK response, so the `figma` responder 404s a malformed key, the injected shell command runs and reads a planted witness file whose content the simplifier echoes back, the Node `fetch` trusting the cert through `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` and the curl child through `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`. Settlements. facebook-ads: `host.has_network` true (record A true, record B false; the SSRF reaches the responder); `launch` `--fb-token dummy` (record B carried the token, record A none; the server scans `sys.argv` for it at startup and fetch_pagination_url never reads it); `benign_calls` a `graph.facebook.com` pagination fetch (record A gave one, record B none); `declared_function_is_effect` false (record B over-marked it true; the out-of-host fetch the vulnerable version follows is the flaw, not the declared next-page function, the apify precedent); and `backend_need` corrected from `external` to `fixture` (both records external; `graph.facebook.com` is a public API a provisioned responder stands in for, the apify precedent). figma: `launch` `--stdio` (record A empty, record B `--figma-api-key <key> --stdio`; the API key rides as the `FIGMA_API_KEY` env the backend sets, and the launch carries the transport flag the server needs to speak stdio rather than start an HTTP listener); `host.has_network` true (both records false; the curl child reaches the responder); `backend_need` corrected from `external` to `fixture` (both records external; `api.figma.com` is a public API a provisioned responder stands in for); and `tools` narrowed to `get_figma_data` only (both records also listed `download_figma_images`, whose `fileKey` is already `^[a-zA-Z0-9]+$` in the vulnerable 0.6.2 schema, so it cannot carry the shell injection on either arm). Both records already agreed on `command_execution` and `declared_function_is_effect` false for figma, so no class or dfie settlement was recorded, though the plan anticipated one. Both calibrate green under `run_nuisance calibrate`. No run or gate has taken place yet, so no figure is produced.

- 2026-11-02: version 3 of the rule, which brings the fixtures under the behavior of the software they replace. A tool whose benign call cannot return without error on the real software it drives, or whose software no distribution channel still offers, is not a counted tool, with the run, the capture or the source line that shows it, and neither is a tool whose failure comes from the container and cannot be repaired under the confinement, and the two admission agents record that field like the others. The declared function is defined by the action of the flaw's class on what a tool argument carries or names, which writes down how the admissions so far settled it. A stand-in answers from the real software when it runs under the confinement, else from a capture, from the software's own documentation, fixtures, tests or API examples, then from the server's tests, and an output invented without a source is recorded as such. A fixture seeds a published sample when one exists. Nothing the fixture serves, seeds or passes to the server carries a value of the machine that runs the suite, a value in the witness's format or a word written for the fixture about the audit, and no stand-in relays what the flaw delivers to it, which binds the fix check and a reused holdout calibration too. A run's networks are closed as ADR 034 decides. The maintainer may repair fixtures until the auditor first audits any nuisance fixture, which bounds the repairs logged on 2026-11-01 after the fact: those were made before this clause existed, they stay within its window and its list of what the maintainer may repair, and they are checked against the version 3 rules of Admission like every fixture. A benign call whose result text reports a failure now fails. The Docker exit counts a remote service as missing only when no stand-in can replace it. The fixture fingerprint covers every file the patched Dockerfile copies in, what the backend serves, the stand-ins and the seeded samples. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place, and no audit result, trace or figure of this suite was read: the clauses come from a review of every stand-in, backend and seeded file against the software it replaces. The criteria apply again to the servers the walk has reached, and each server that leaves or loses a tool is logged as its admission changes. Commit `06ab00a`.

- 2026-11-03: the code catches up with version 3 of the rule. The admission gains the fields the rule adds: per tool, whether its benign call can return without error on the real software and under the confinement, with the cause and evidence when it cannot (`ToolRecord.real_software`); per benign call, a substring its answer must contain (`expect`) and the prerequisite calls made before it (`before`); per fix check, whether its positive control rests on a stand-in that relays the flaw's effect (`relies_on_relaying_stand_in`); per server, its stand-ins with the source of each output and its seeds with their citation and pin (`stand_ins`, `seeds`, `evals/fixture_provenance.py`). Each new field defaults to `null`, read as not yet recorded under version 3, so the committed admission loads unchanged. That default is a transition that ends in the commit of the re-admission campaign filling the last field, where the fields become required. Load checks refuse a removed tool carrying a verdict, two verdicts for one tool across a server's flaws, an `expect` empty or contained in the call's arguments or its prerequisites', two different benign calls for one tool across counted flaws, and a seed or stand-in output missing its source. A tool whose real software does not answer is not counted, a tool with no record stays counted, and a flaw whose positive control rests on a relaying stand-in does not count. The benign check, which `run_nuisance vet`, the pre-run check and the gate share, now fails when a counted tool or a prerequisite tool is absent from the server's `tools/list`, when a prerequisite call errors, and when an answer lacks its `expect`. `run_nuisance run` and `run_nuisance gate` refuse, exit 3, while a field version 3 adds is unrecorded for an audited server, a flaw that does not count never blocking, and `vet` prints how many servers have such fields. The nuisance fixture fingerprint covers each benign call's `expect` and prerequisite calls, so every server's fingerprint moves once, before any run, and tests require every file a run or backend Dockerfile copies in to be covered by construction (no local `COPY` or `ADD`, a remote `ADD` only with a checksum), every seed pin and stand-in file to sit in content the fingerprint covers. The admission dossier describes the new fields as required, and the kit's `check_fix` prints each benign response, so an agent can read what a benign call answered. No admission value changes, so no tool or server leaves. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place. Commits `0eed522` to `3b9338d` and this entry's.

- 2026-11-03: the nuisance run's confinement gains the memory bound the rule names, `--memory 4g --memory-swap 4g` (`evals/nuisance_confinement.py`), fixed before any run and the same for every server. The value is the smallest bound under which every admitted server starts as its documentation prescribes, plus headroom for an audit's load. arcadedb (`Maven:com.arcadedb:arcadedb-server`) sets its own heap: its image's `mcp-stdio.sh` starts the JVM with `-XX:+UseZGC -Xms2G -Xmx2G`, it peaks at 2159 MiB at start, and under `2g` it is OOM-killed at start on every attempt, while every other server peaks at 316 MiB or less (the server container's cgroup `memory.peak` through start, listing and benign calls, at `ac03d53`). The headroom covers the JVM's off-heap memory under queries and Doris, about 2.14 GiB at rest, under queries. A bound below the software's own fixed heap would take a tool out through a container failure the suite chose, which the rule does not allow. This departs from the product's confined default of `2g`, which would not start arcadedb for a user either. The bound applies to the server container of every nuisance launch (`run`, `vet`, `gate` and the benign probe) and to every backend container, the real software (the Doris cluster, MySQL, Neo4j) and the stand-ins (the responders, the apify stand-in, the moto stub) alike: a persistent backend found running without it is removed and started again with it, once. It does not apply to the fix checks' server containers and per-launch responder containers, which keep the holdout's confinement: the criterion "under the confinement" is checked on the run's launch by the vet and the probe, and a fix check sends no resource-abuse payload, so the bound would check nothing there. The shared singleton responders a fix check ensures are backend containers and carry it. - 2026-11-08: the version 4 admission campaign applied the readings below of the rule's existing text on every server, so no version of the rule opens, and each server a reading touches is recorded with its admission. An evaluator whose fix removes only the escape: a tool that evaluates the expression or runs the code an argument carries, whose fix restricts what it evaluates (a restricted parser, a sandbox, a runner) without removing the evaluation, still does the action of its class on what the argument carries, so its declared function is the effect whichever description is read, and the fix does not remove the capability in the sense of The declared function. The settlements of 2026-10-31 that read such a fix as removing the capability no longer hold. A dispatcher, a tool that runs any registered tool an argument names with arguments an argument carries, declares the union of the actions of the tools it can dispatch to. A root the server holds from its configuration (an environment variable, a launch option, a documented default) in force under the run's launch is a root the server declares: reading a path inside it is the fixed operation the rule gives as its example, and the declared function is not the effect. A directory an argument names is not such a root. For `internal_fetch`, an argument that carries the base URL the tool fetches makes the declared function the effect, and an argument that only selects records (a project filter) does not. A tool is a tool of a flaw when an argument it receives reaches the faulty code, and a tool the advisory names with no argument leading there is not. A tool that writes, moves or lists through the faulty guard of a `read_outside_scope` flaw, with a path the caller chooses, is a tool of the flaw, and its declared function is not the effect, the action of the class being reading. The image installs the server as its documentation says, keeping a lock the project ships when the documented command uses it. When the documented install no longer starts, the fixture adds the loosest bound on the single package at fault, preferably one the upstream project publishes (such as `mcp<2`), recorded with the run that shows the failure without it, and the whole tree is never frozen. A flaw that no source and no release shows fixed does not set the version of its server: it is recorded and its tools are not counted, as the fix-check clause says, and the server stays on its other flaws. A counted tool that also carries an open flaw whose class's categories overlap leaves the count, removed in data as `adb_shell` was.

Its two tools, `fast_read_file` and `fast_read_multiple_files`, read through the identical `safePath` check and carry a `read_outside_scope` category that overlaps the server's counted `command_execution` flaw, so both leave the flaw's `tools`, where the committed admission listed `fast_read_file` uncounted; `fast_read_multiple_files`, which both records add to the flaw, is not listed either. The benign call is `fast_get_disk_usage` with `path` `/`, which both records wrote the same and which answers under the confinement with the server's own computed `use_percentage` field, its expected text; the records differed only on the dropped read calls, so no read call remains, and `run_nuisance vet` answers it. The fix check runs its own module (source `fix_check`, no holdout copy): `run_nuisance calibrate --cve CVE-2026-5327` surfaces the witness on the vulnerable version and not on the patched one, the patched benign call answering, `relies_on_relaying_stand_in` false, the server reading the witness through `df` with no stand-in. Settled: `fast_get_disk_usage`'s declared function not the effect (the guard-rail limb of the unified principle: the fix removes the capability); the two read tools left out of the count; the benign call; and the seed, kept as none. Record A proposed the package's own `package.json` at `/home` and record B its `README.md` under `/home/library`, each an in-scope file for the read benign calls; those calls leave with their tools, so no counted call reads a seed, and under the reading that keeps the conforming seed writing the fewest values the fixture keeps none. Accepted departure from the rule's letter: `df` runs from coreutils `9.1`, the `coreutils` package of Debian 12 (bookworm), the distribution of the digest-pinned base `node:20-slim`, rather than at the stable release current on the day; the server documentation names no `df` version or channel, `df` being a standard system utility present in the base image. The first admission's settlements go, this admission settling them anew; the two records agree on the real software, the declared function, the fix check, the host (the unprivileged account, working directory `/tmp`, no network), the launch, the stand-ins (none) and the software release. Record B was launched on `claude-opus-5-5` and answered on `claude-opus-4-8`, its `fallback_model`; record A was launched on and answered on `claude-opus-4-8`. Record B's recorded fix check quoted a witness of its own run, which no fixture copies. The server's fixture fingerprint moves, `a4a9bf294439d326b795a6645e29165e8f1b111f8069acdec450b8c7eabc8b71` to `90b44a63360e0952e68117ce52393d7169cb2404ca8461784250f8dfef2985df`, from the benign call's expected text now recorded; no other admitted server's moves. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
