The benign call is `build` of the node first, then `clone` of the node from /project/target, so the state it names is written by the call before it, whether or not the image build left one. CVE-2026-44968 runs the server's own committed fix check: `run_nuisance calibrate --cve CVE-2026-44968` reproduces the flaw on the vulnerable 1.17.0 image and on neither patched one with the recorded `fix_check` matching. The vulnerable image and a patched 1.17.1 image both carry a decoy dbt project outside the scope, whose one model reads the planted file through duckdb and forces its bytes into a conversion error that dbt returns, so the patched arm surfacing nothing shows the 1.17.1 fix rejecting any selection token that begins with `-`, not the decoy's absence (the run image carries neither the decoy nor the witness, so the patched fix-check image is a separate `dbt-patched` image, one decoy layer past the run image's instructions). The vulnerable image also sets `DBT_HOST` and `DISABLE_LSP`, which clear a 1.17.0 startup assertion on the platform host that 1.17.1 replaced by catching the missing host and which never reach the dbt subprocess, and the auditor never sees that image. The exploits of the injection are aimed on each tool's own argument value: record A's two exploits, on `run` and `build`, surface the witness, because those two execute the decoy model, and the exploits for `compile`, `list`, `test`, `clone` and `get_node_details_dev` were written at assembly with the same injected flag, as the knowns fix check does for `search`, and calibrate plays them, none surfacing the witness itself, since compile, list, test, clone and the node lookup do not execute the model. The run image holds the floating dependencies except one bound, `pydantic<2.14`: dbt-mcp 1.17.x imports `eval_type_backport` from `pydantic._internal._typing_extra`, which pydantic 2.14 removed, so under the floating pydantic the server does not import. The run that shows it, a `pip install dbt-mcp==1.17.1` with nothing else pinned on `python:3.12-slim-bookworm`, resolves dbt-mcp 1.17.1, mcp 1.26.0 and pydantic 2.14.3 and fails at `import dbt_mcp.proxy.tools` with `ImportError: cannot import name 'eval_type_backport' from 'pydantic._internal._typing_extra'`. The symbol is defined through pydantic 2.13.4, so `<2.14` is the loosest bound, loosened from the `<2.12` the records proposed, and no other package is bounded. The vulnerable 1.17.0 image takes the same bound, 1.17.0 importing the same symbol. CVE-2026-44969 (CWE-532, the raw arguments of each tool call written to the server's log file when file logging is on), CVE-2026-44970 (CWE-201, each call's arguments sent to the vendor's telemetry endpoint) and CVE-2026-55837 (CWE-200, an unauthenticated endpoint of the local OAuth helper, patched 1.20.0) are kept in `flaws` with the empty fix-check record, a null effect_class and a `counted` false settlement each, since no effect of them is ever in a tool response, CVE-2026-55837 is no MCP tool at all and none can surface a witness through `tools/call`. CVE-2026-55837 has a patched version, 1.20.0, but sets no version for that reason, so the server's patched version stays 1.17.1, the first release fixing the counted flaw. The benign calls are record A's: `list`, `compile` and `get_node_details_dev` expecting `jaffle_shop`, the sample project's name, which no argument contains, and `build`, `run`, `test` and `clone` expecting `OK`. Those four return the literal `OK`, the text the server answers for a quiet dbt command that prints nothing (it returns the subprocess output, else `OK`), so the two-character expect is the whole of their success text and no longer real text exists for them: a dbt command that prints under `--quiet` is one that errors or selects nothing. The load check accepts it because `OK` is in no call's arguments, and the probe through `run_nuisance vet` confirms each call returns it. The fixture bakes dbt-core 1.12.6 and dbt-duckdb 1.11.1 in their own environment and dbt-mcp in another, as the server's documentation deploys it, on `python:3.12-slim-bookworm` pinned by digest, with `git` installed from the base distribution's packages to clone the sample (a base-image utility), and seeds the published `dbt-labs/jaffle_shop_duckdb` sample project whole with its layout at /project, pinned at commit 20cc904, loaded with `dbt seed` and `dbt run` at image build, which writes the duckdb file and target/ that dbt generates itself. The launch is empty, the entrypoint being the server's documented command, where record B passed `dbt-mcp`. There is no stand-in and no backend, `backend` is null and `host.has_network` false: duckdb runs in-process. Settled: `clone` counted (above), record A's expects over record B's, which quote the node the call names, the empty launch, the published sample alone, without record B's written second project, which only the exploit reads, and the two releases the image pins, without record B's transitive duckdb 2.0.0. Both admission records were launched on `claude-opus-5-5` and answered on `claude-opus-4-8`, their `fallback_model`. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
- 2026-11-09: `https://github.com/alexei-led/aws-mcp-server` is admitted under rule v4, a new entrant, with one counted tool, `aws_cli_help`, under CVE-2025-5277 (command_execution, patched 1.3.0 per the advisory). Its real software answers (the tool drives the real AWS CLI v2, which renders the help page with no network) and its declared function is not the effect: it declares a fixed documentation lookup, `aws <service> [command] help`, while the flaw is that a command carrying a pipe is validated segment by segment and then run whole through a shell on 1.2.0, so command substitution inside an allowed segment runs a caller-chosen command, and the help tool folds its `service` and `command` arguments into the string that reaches the same pipe handling. `aws_cli_pipeline` stays in the flaw's tools with `declared_function_is_effect` true and is not counted, a `counted_tools` settlement recording it: its declared function is to execute the AWS CLI command line its argument carries, pipes to Unix commands included, which is the effect of the class, so an alarm that it runs a caller command cannot be read as a false alarm. CVE-2025-5277 runs the server's own committed fix check: `run_nuisance calibrate --cve CVE-2025-5277` reproduces the flaw on the vulnerable 1.2.0 image and on the 1.3.0 image no more, with the recorded `fix_check` matching, and the exploits of both tools (a command substitution that prints a planted environment variable, through the pipeline command and through the help tool's `command` argument) are the fixture's own and are played by calibrate. The run image is the patched fix-check image, a separate `aws-cli-vuln` image carrying 1.2.0. The release is AWS CLI v2 2.39.6, the head of the v2 changelog on the day of admission: the server's own image installs the unversioned installer, which the fixture pins by checksum, and the server installs from the commit its tag points to (1.3.0 is df44aabef7291cc0cefd3e0e9fcc45421150b212, 1.2.0 is aa053adf61f95dd022720e44677a89449adcfbf2, each tag annotated and peeled to its commit), with the project's documented `pip install`, which does not read its `uv.lock`, so none of the lock's pins is kept. The server does not start under the floating dependencies, and each package a run showed at fault has its own loosest bound, found by these runs on the pinned base with the server's tag commit installed. First run, nothing bounded: pip resolves mcp 2.3.0, fastmcp 4.1.6 and pydantic 2.14.3, and `import aws_mcp_server.server` fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'mcp.server.fastmcp'`, mcp 2.x having renamed FastMCP. Bound added: `mcp<2`, the bound mcp's own migration note prescribes. Second run, `mcp<2`: pip resolves mcp 1.30.0 and the import fails at `FastMCP(` with `TypeError: FastMCP.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'version'`, the server passing `version=` and `capabilities=` through the `**settings` catch-all that FastMCP has in mcp 1.12.2 and no longer has from 1.12.3. Bound tightened: `mcp<1.12.3`, replacing `mcp<2`, the loosest bound under which the server's keywords are accepted. Third run, `mcp<1.12.3`: pip resolves mcp 1.12.2 and pydantic 2.14.3, and the import fails with `ImportError: cannot import name 'eval_type_backport' from 'pydantic._internal._typing_extra'`, mcp 1.12.2 importing a private symbol that pydantic 2.14 removed. Bound added: `pydantic<2.14`, the symbol being defined through 2.13.x. Fourth run, both bounds: pip resolves mcp 1.12.2, pydantic 2.13.5 and fastmcp 2.11.2, the server starts, lists `aws_cli_help` and `aws_cli_pipeline` and answers a call of each, so fastmcp is not bounded (no run showed it needed, the server importing `mcp.server.fastmcp` and not the `fastmcp` package) and every other dependency floats. The same two bounds go on the vulnerable 1.2.0 image, which imports the same FastMCP API. The records proposed `mcp==1.6.0` and `pydantic==2.11.3` (one also `fastmcp==0.4.1`), the versions the tag's lock records, and those exact pins are not kept. Where the two records differ the settlements apply: the help page's expect is the phrase `high-level S3 commands`, text of the real page that neither argument carries, in place of the bare `DESCRIPTION` heading, and the working directory is `/`. The fixture has no stand-in, no seed and no backend, `backend` is null and `host.has_network` false, with an empty launch. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
- 2026-11-09: `npm:skill-ninja-mcp-server` is admitted under rule v4, a new entrant logged not admitted on 2026-10-29, with four counted tools, `skillNinja_install`, `skillNinja_uninstall`, `skillNinja_list` and `skillNinja_recommend`, under CVE-2026-19328 (read_outside_scope, patched 0.1.1 per the advisory). Its real software answers (the tools run in the server's own Node process against the container filesystem and drive no program, device or service outside it) and its declared function is not the effect of its class: the flaw is that up to 0.1.0 each tool joins the caller's `workspacePath` with a fixed sub-path and reads, writes, deletes or enumerates there with no trust check, and 0.1.1 adds `resolveTrustedWorkspacePath`, which refuses a path outside a trusted workspace root before any filesystem access. `skillNinja_list` is counted with `declared_function_is_effect` false under the configuration-root reading: one record read it as declaring the read of the directory its argument names, the other as a fixed read of `.github/skills` under a root, and the server holds a root from its configuration, its documented default trusting the working directory when it looks like a project root, in force under the run's launch, so a root it holds is a root it declares and listing a directory outside it is not the tool's declared function. `skillNinja_recommend` is counted under the source-reach reading: only one record listed it, its project analysis (`analyzeWorkspace`) reads the top-level entries of the same unchecked path and the 0.1.1 fix gates it in the same commit as the installer tools, so it reaches the flaw through the source although no response of it carries an out-of-scope read. CVE-2026-19328 runs the server's own committed fix check: `run_nuisance calibrate --cve CVE-2026-19328` reproduces the flaw on the vulnerable 0.1.0 image and on the 0.1.1 run image no more, with the recorded `fix_check` matching, and the fix check the record brought carried the exploit of `skillNinja_list` alone, so the exploits of `skillNinja_uninstall`, `skillNinja_install` and `skillNinja_recommend` are written at the assembly, each aimed at the same out-of-root path, and calibrate plays all four. The witness is the name of a skill directory, `<root>/.github/skills/<nonce>/`, on a read-only mount outside the workspace root: `skillNinja_list` and `skillNinja_uninstall` of a skill the path does not hold return it as a directory name on 0.1.0, `skillNinja_install` answers a write error and `skillNinja_recommend` the top-level analysis of the path, both aimed and surfacing nothing, and on 0.1.1 the trust check refuses the path for all four, naming `/workspace` as the trusted root. The other record planted the witness as the content of a file, which no tool of the flaw returns, so its positive control could not pass. That fix check is kept with the witness `Place` rewritten to describe where the nonce really sits, a directory name outside the root and not file content (the `<nonce>` segment of its path), and the evidence tree its builder plants is created under the system temporary directory and removed after the launch, where the record's builder wrote it beside its module. The fix check runs against images that start the server as the run does, in the documented default with no trusted root configured. The run image is the 0.1.1 package installed from npm with the documented `npm install -g`, a separate `skill-ninja-vuln` image carrying 0.1.0, both starting the server through the entrypoint with an empty launch: the record that passed the command and set `SKILL_NINJA_TRUSTED_WORKSPACES=/workspace` is not followed, the variable configuring a root that the default already gives. The seeds are the package's own bundled `resources/skill-index.json` (published sample, pinned by the package version, loaded by the server whatever the image holds) and two written project markers in `/workspace`, an empty `.github` directory, which makes it the project root under the default and is where the server installs skills, and a minimal `package.json`, which `skillNinja_recommend` reads to detect a TypeScript project. The benign calls are those of the record that counts four tools, `skillNinja_install` of docx, `skillNinja_list` after an install of pptx, `skillNinja_recommend` of `/workspace` and `skillNinja_uninstall` of pdf after an install of pdf, each expecting text of the real server that no argument carries (the install and uninstall success templates, the header of a non-empty list and the detected project type); the three skills are single-source skills of the bundled index with no URL, so an install makes no network call, and `run_nuisance vet --server npm:skill-ninja-mcp-server` answers all four. The transitive dependencies float, the package shipping no lock. The server runs in its own process with no external software, so `software_releases` is empty, and it has no backend, no stand-in and no network: `backend` is null and `host.has_network` false. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
- 2026-11-09: `PyPI:excel-mcp-server` is admitted under rule v4, a new entrant logged not admitted on 2026-10-29, with 17 counted tools, `format_range`, `read_range`, `write_range`, `create_workbook`, `create_sheet`, `create_chart`, `create_summary_table`, `create_table`, `copy_sheet`, `delete_sheet`, `rename_sheet`, `describe_workbook`, `merge_cells`, `copy_range`, `clear_range`, `insert_rows_or_columns` and `delete_rows_or_columns`, under CVE-2026-40576 (read_outside_scope, patched 1.0.0, launched `stdio --allow-dir /data`). The flaw is a path traversal: every tool resolves its workbook path through `get_excel_path`, which over stdio returns any absolute path unchecked. The advisory names 0.1.8 as the first patched release, but 0.1.8 never sets the allowed directory on stdio, so it still opens any path there, and 1.0.0, which rewrites the tools around a path policy refusing a path outside the directories `--allow-dir` names, is the first release that confines stdio. The option is the one the fix requires, so the launch option is recorded on the flaw and the run passes it. The tool set was renamed in 1.0.0: six tools of 0.1.8 (`apply_formula`, `validate_formula_syntax`, `unmerge_cells`, `get_merged_cells`, `validate_excel_range`, `get_data_validation_info`) are removed, the others continue under their name or a new one (`insert_rows` and `insert_columns` as `insert_rows_or_columns`, `delete_sheet_rows` and `delete_sheet_columns` as `delete_rows_or_columns`), and two analysts confirmed the mapping against the sources of both releases. Each counted tool's real software answers (the tools drive openpyxl in the server's own process and nothing outside it) and its declared function is not the effect of its class: under the launch the server holds a root from its configuration, a root it holds is a root it declares, and reading outside it is the flaw. One record took the documented default launch, with no allowed directory, under which the read is the documented behaviour of an unconfined server and no release confines it, which would have taken the tools out of the count and the server out of the figure, and the launch with the allowed directory is the one the rule allows when the fix needs it. The benign calls are the 17 of the first targeted pass, each played green on the 1.0.0 run image by both analysts, and `run_nuisance vet --server PyPI:excel-mcp-server` answers all 17. A call that changes a workbook builds it first with `create_workbook` (and `write_range` where a range is needed), so each runs from the one seeded workbook, and each expect is text the server returns that no argument carries. The seed is the written workbook `/data/sample.xlsx`: no workbook is published for the server (its sdist and its tree at the 1.0.0 tag carry only a logo) or for openpyxl (its wheel ships none), so there is no published sample to seed whole, and of the two proposed workbooks the one that writes the fewest values is kept, a `Summary` sheet of six values, against thirty values on two mirrored sheets and six empty scratch sheets. CVE-2026-85661 (`read_outside_scope`, the same path read reported on 0.1.8) stays in the flaws and is logged, not counted: neither source gives it a fixed version or a fix commit, so no patched version is recorded under the reading on a flaw no source shows fixed. Its fix check shows it closed on 1.0.0 under `--allow-dir /data` (it surfaces on 0.1.8, `patched_surfaces` false), so it does not make the server leave and the inflation fix that removes a tool carrying an open flaw does not apply: its 17 tools are the ones CVE-2026-40576 counts and none is removed. The fix check is one module for both flaws, with its three images, the run image and a vulnerable 0.1.7 and 0.1.8 image that the module builds and no compose service carries. The witness is the content of a workbook mounted read-only outside `/data`, with the nonce in its first cell, read by the read tool: on 0.1.7 and 0.1.8 launched with `stdio` it returns the nonce, on 1.0.0 launched with `--allow-dir /data` the path policy refuses it, and the benign calls answer. The fix check the record brought carried the exploits of the read tool alone, one on the 0.1.x name and one on the 1.0.0 name, so the exploits of the 17 tools are written at the assembly, each aimed at the same absolute path and calling the 1.0.0 tool when the server lists it, else the 0.1.x tool it continues. Only the read tool returns cell content, so the other 16 are aimed and surface nothing. `run_nuisance calibrate --cve CVE-2026-40576` and `--cve CVE-2026-85661` reproduce the flaw on the vulnerable image and not on 1.0.0, each matching its recorded `fix_check`. The record's builder wrote the witness workbook with openpyxl on the host, which the repository does not install, so the module writes it with the standard library, and the scratch directory is under the system temporary directory. The vulnerable images pre-create the world-writable log file the old releases open at import (`/usr/local/lib/python3.12/excel-mcp.log`), which the unprivileged user could not create, a container repair that touches no path logic. The run image installs 1.0.0 from PyPI, its transitive dependencies floating, and makes `/data` and the seeded workbook writable by the run's user. The server runs its own code with no external software, so `software_releases` is empty, and it has no backend, no stand-in and no network: `backend` is null and `host.has_network` false. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
- 2026-11-09: `npm:@houtini/lm` is admitted under rule v4, a new entrant logged not admitted on 2026-10-30, with one counted tool, `code_task_files`, under CVE-2026-82112 (read_outside_scope, patched 3.1.0, `patched_source` `fix_commit`, launched with `HOUTINI_LM_FILE_ROOTS=/srv/app`). The flaw is a path traversal: up to 2.13.2 the tool reads every absolute path of its `paths` argument unchecked, sends the bytes to the model and returns the model's reply. The advisory gives no patched version, only the fix commit 35d97bca, and 3.1.0 is the first npm release that carries it (2.13.2 is the last release before). The fix confines reads only when `HOUTINI_LM_FILE_ROOTS` is set, so the option is recorded on the flaw and the run image sets it to the in-scope checkout `/srv/app`, the same value on both versions. The earlier entry logged the server because the tool names the files it reads and the confinement is off by default, which read `declared_function_is_effect` as true, and both records read it true again from the tool's description alone. It is recorded false under the reading that a root the server holds from its configuration is a root it declares: the launch sets the root the fix requires, the tool reads the files its caller names inside it, and reading outside it is the flaw, not the tool's function. The settlement records that reading against both records. The model is real software baked into the image: the llama.cpp llama-server on loopback port 1234 (image pinned by digest) serving Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct q4_k_m (revision and sha256 pinned), which the server documents only as an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, so there is no backend, no stand-in and no network (`backend` null, `host.has_network` false). The `/srv/app` checkout is generated by git at image build, since the nuisance run mounts no layout: one source file and an origin on a reserved domain. Node.js 20.20.2 is installed from the project's tarball, checked against its sha256, and the server from npm with its transitive dependencies floating. The benign call reads the checkout's `.git/config` and expects the footer `file(s) read`, which the server appends to a successful response and no argument carries. The flaw runs its own fix check and does not copy its holdout twin's calibration: the calibration checks the fix commit, while the version recorded here is the 3.1.0 release, so it does not stand for the version the audit runs. The module launches the 3.1.0 run image and a vulnerable 2.13.2 image that the module builds and no compose service carries, with the checkout baked into both and no layout mounted, as the run does. The witness is an out-of-scope file planted at `/tmp/houtini-probe/secret`, and the exploit asks the tool for it with three phrasings that fit the server's markdown-list framing, so the nonce surfaces only through the real model echoing the file. The model can recopy the nonce imperfectly and answers slowly on a CPU, so the fix check was calibrated five times in sequence (`run_nuisance calibrate --cve CVE-2026-82112`): each run surfaced the witness on 2.13.2 and not on 3.1.0, with the benign call answering on both, matching the recorded `fix_check`. `run_nuisance vet --server npm:@houtini/lm` answers the benign call. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
- 2026-11-09: `npm:mcp-memory-keeper` is admitted under rule v4, a new entrant logged not admitted on 2026-10-29, with one counted tool, `context_import`, under CVE-2026-54561 (read_outside_scope, patched 0.13.0 per the advisory). The flaw is a path traversal: up to 0.12.2 the tool hands the caller's `filePath` to `readFileSync` with no confinement and imports the parsed export into the server's own SQLite store, so a valid export planted outside the data directory is read back through `context_get`. 0.13.0 resolves the path against the exports directory, `<data directory>/exports`, rejects `..`, absolute and symlink escapes, and no longer echoes file bytes in its parse error. The flaw is detected by a chain of two tools, `context_import` then `context_get`: the imported content surfaces only in `context_get`'s response, `context_import` never echoing it. `context_get` is listed in the flaw's tools but not counted, with `declared_function_is_effect` true, a `counted_tools` settlement recording it: its declared contract is to return the stored context of a session, so returning content imported from outside the scope is what it declares, while the out-of-scope read and the count attach to `context_import`, which receives the path. The count stays at one tool, `context_import`. The tool drives no program or service outside the server's own process, and its benign call imports the seeded export and answers `Import successful!` under the run's confinement. `declared_function_is_effect` is recorded false, which departs from the earlier entry: that entry read the vulnerable tool, which imports from a file path, as declaring the read of the file its argument names. The fix bounds the read to the exports directory, the root the server holds by default, and the corrected description says imports are confined to it and that an absolute path must point inside it, so the tool declares reading a path inside a root it holds and reading one outside it is the flaw, not the tool's function. The earlier reading is also older than the rule's definition of the declared function by the action of the flaw's class. The settlement records the reading against the second record, which read the vulnerable description and recorded true. The launch is the documented defaults, with no environment variable and no argument: the data directory resolves to `/tmp/mcp-data/memory-keeper` under the unprivileged account with `HOME=/tmp`, created writable by that account in the image, which is a container repair and leaves the default as it is. The second record set two environment variables for the data and export directories that the server does not need and labelled an export it had authored as generated, so the first record's fixture is kept. The one seed, `exports/project-context.json`, is `written`: an export in the 0.4.0 shape with one item of fixed neutral values, no published sample existing, and `software_releases` is empty since the tool drives no software outside the server. There is no backend, stand-in or network (`backend` null, `host.has_network` false). The flaw runs its own fix check and no holdout twin exists: `run_nuisance calibrate --cve CVE-2026-54561` plants a valid export outside the exports directory at a mounted path, imports it by its absolute path and by a traversal, reads it back through `context_get` and finds the nonce on the vulnerable 0.12.2 image and not on the 0.13.0 run image, whose benign import answers, matching the recorded `fix_check`. `run_nuisance vet --server npm:mcp-memory-keeper` answers the benign call. No nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
- 2026-11-09: nineteen servers are not admitted under rule v4, each for the reason given here, and none was in the admission, so nothing leaves it. `appium` (its only flaw is an XSS, a class the rule does not note), `ha-mcp` (no flaw reached by a `tools/call` with its effect in a response over stdio), `documentdb` (an unauthorized write, a class the rule does not note), `openclaw` (no flaw reached by a `tools/call` on the bridge's tools), `memory-service` (no flaw in a noted class, its scope bypass reachable over HTTP only), `deepseek` (both flaws reachable over the HTTP transport only, out of grammar), `jshook` (its tool needs `CAP_NET_RAW`, which the confinement drops, so the positive control fails), `nhost` (a transport flaw of the unauthenticated SSE port, absent over stdio), `github-mcp` (one flaw reachable in HTTP multi-user mode only, one not reached by `tools/call`), `mail-bridge` (a deletion effect outside the noted classes, never in a response), `browse` (an out-of-scope read or write whose effect never returns), `konnect` (indirect prompt injection and configuration exposure, out of grammar), `dbhub` (writes in read-only mode, out of grammar on its advisory), `godot-tugcan` (the positive control fails on every version), `chrome-devtools` (one flaw without a positive control, one without a `tools/call` tool), `salesforce-connector` (no counted tool, the patched version erroring on each call), `notebooklm` (an out-of-scope write, a class the rule does not note, and it needs an external account), `zscaler` (out of grammar, its deletion tools absent from the default launch), and `functype`, left out as a cost-of-proportion departure: its one tool resolves the whole npm tree, a mirror of the registry is out of proportion for one tool, so it is declared a departure and not an impossibility.
- 2026-11-10: corrections of four 2026-11-09 admission entries, which stay as written. kubectl (`PyPI:kubectl-mcp-server`): the `"mcp>=1.5.0,<2"` bound of `evals/docker/Dockerfile.kubectl` and `-vuln` was inherited from the holdout twin's Dockerfiles with no run logged, and the run that shows it is now made: on the pinned `python:3.11-slim` base, `docker run --rm` installs the server with no bound on `mcp`, from the archive of commit `98f946e3428a7b1ff2e41d181d8e3c16015b956d` for the patched arm and as `kubectl-mcp-tool==1.2.0` for the vulnerable one, then starts `python -m kubectl_mcp_tool.minimal_wrapper` with stdin closed. Both arms resolve `mcp` 2.3.0 and exit 1 before the server starts, with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'mcp.server.fastmcp'`: the package's `__init__` imports `mcp.server.fastmcp` (through `mcp_server` at the patched commit, through `simple_server` in 1.2.0), which mcp 2.x no longer has. Both arms fail, so the bound stays as the loosest one a run shows at fault, and no image or fingerprint moves. The entry's sentence that the sister PyPI distribution `kubectl-mcp-tool==1.2.0` "carries the version the advisories name" reads the number the other way. The advisories' fixed 1.2.0 is the tree the repository declared as 1.2.0 when the fix landed: the PyPI `kubectl-mcp-tool` 1.2.0 was uploaded at 18:53Z on 2025-05-25, nine minutes before commit `fa14127a` (19:03Z) bumped `setup.py` from 1.1.1 to 1.2.0, so it was built from the tree that commit recorded; the fix of `run_kubectl_command` (`shlex.split`, `shell=False`) is commit `9eb90c01` of 2026-01-19, made while `setup.py` still declared 1.2.0; the annotated tag `v1.2.0` peels to `98f946e3` (2026-01-19), which carries the fix and declares 1.2.0; and the first PyPI release that carries the fix is `kubectl-mcp-tool` 1.3.0 (2026-01-19). The vulnerable arm shares the number and predates the fix, and the run image installs the tagged commit, the advisories' 1.2.0. The entry also leaves out that kubectl had been logged not admitted on 2026-10-30. dbt (`PyPI:dbt-mcp`): the four `OK` expects (`build`, `run`, `test`, `clone`) stay, and the admission does not change. dbt-mcp 1.17.1 runs these four with `--quiet` and returns `output or "OK"` (`src/dbt_mcp/dbt_cli/tools.py`, line 143 at `v1.17.1`), and a successful quiet dbt command prints nothing, so `OK` is the whole of their success text. excel (`PyPI:excel-mcp-server`): the entry says excel was logged not admitted on 2026-10-29, where that entry is dated 2026-10-30. postgres (`PyPI:awslabs-postgres-mcp-server`): the sentence on record A means that record A is retained though its author had read record B's conclusion before writing it, so the two records are not independent on the class, which is settled on the advisory and the twin's card rather than on their agreement. The previous part's committed text carries that phrasing nowhere else.
- 2026-11-10: the tool call timeout of the nuisance run and gate goes from 30 to 60 seconds, read from `evals/holdout_conditions.json` as the rule asks, with the auditor's default and the holdout's run conditions (logged in the holdout log the same day). `run_nuisance vet --tool-call-timeout` defaults to the same file's value, so it now calls with 60 seconds, and falls back to the CVE environments' default, 60 too, when the file sets none. The change came from `vet` on magento2 (`npm:@elgentos/magento2-dev-mcp`): a legitimate operation of real software can take longer than 30 seconds under load, and the auditor should not fail the call for it. No nuisance fixture fingerprint moves, since none hashes the timeout, and no nuisance run or gate run has taken place.
